Abstract: Reed-Solomon proximity gaps establish soundness guarantees for protocols that test received words through random combinations. For length-$n$ codes over $\mathbb{F}_q$, classical scalar combiners achieve error $O(n/q)$ in the unique-decoding regime, with larger known bounds near the Johnson radius.
  We prove smaller error bounds using affine combiners, which combine two received words $f,g$ as $f(x)+(α+βx)g(x)$, where $α,β$ are independent uniform elements of $\mathbb{F}_q$. For output degree bound $d=ρn$, fixed $ρ\in(0,1)$ and $0<η<1-\sqrtρ$, and sufficiently large block length $n$, we prove mutual correlated agreement (MCA) with one coordinate of agreement loss at threshold $A=\lceil(\sqrtρ+η)n\rceil$, with error $O_{ρ,η}\left(\frac{1}{q}+\frac{n^2}{q^2}\right)$. The result holds over arbitrary evaluation domains in every finite field, including prime and binary extension fields. The agreement threshold can approach the Johnson threshold, beyond unique decoding.
  For $q\ge n^2$, our error is $O_{ρ,η}(1/q)$, an asymptotic factor-$n$ separation from scalar combiners: we prove that their worst-case MCA error is $Ω_{ρ,η}(n/q)$ at the same agreement threshold and with the same one-coordinate loss. We also prove that affine combiners incur worst-case error $Ω_{ρ,η}(n/q)$ when no agreement loss is allowed. Thus one coordinate of agreement loss is necessary for our guarantee; this relaxation of standard MCA reduces the guaranteed common agreement fraction by at most $1/n$. We also extend the result to $k$-variate Reed-Muller codes on subfield grids, obtaining MCA error $O_{ρ,η,k}(q^{-k})$ for fixed $ρ,η,k$, sufficiently large $n$, and $q\ge n^{k+1}$, with agreement loss $n^{1-1/k}$.

</details>

Abstract: Semantic communication (SemCom) has emerged as a promising paradigm for bandwidth-constrained mobile and vehicular networks. This paper proposes a unified representation learning framework for multimodal SemCom (MSC), which encodes heterogeneous data into a compact semantic space for efficient transmission and source recovery. The framework addresses the multimodal semantic representation gap, which arises when at least one modality contains semantic information that cannot be fully inferred from the other. To preserve this information for reconstruction, the framework decomposes each representation into shared and modality-specific components. We formulate a mutual information objective for source reconstruction, cross-modal alignment of the shared components, and decoupling of the shared and modality-specific components. To enhance transmission efficiency, we introduce a shared knowledge base implemented as a compact codebook of semantic vectors learned during training. The shared components are transmitted digitally as codebook indices, while the modality-specific components are sent in analog form. We apply knowledge distillation during training, using a pretrained vision foundation model to guide the learning of quantized shared features. On image--text pairs over additive white Gaussian noise (AWGN) and Extended Vehicular A (EVA) channels, the proposed framework improves image reconstruction over conventional separate source-channel coding (SSCC) and representative multimodal and unimodal SemCom systems. Further evaluations show gradual degradation under channel-estimation mismatch, complementary effects of alignment, decoupling, and knowledge distillation on the shared representation, and alignment of shared features across modalities with separation of the modality-specific features.

</details>

Abstract: Collaborative perception in Vehicle-to-Everything (V2X) networks faces dual challenges: strictly limited bandwidth and channel impairments. Existing static quantization schemes often suffer from feature manifold drift caused by highly non-stationary traffic environments. To address this, we propose MetaCom, a robust digital semantic communication framework featuring a Meta-Codebook Learning paradigm. First, we introduce a Differentiable Quantized Semantic Communications (DQSemCom) module trained with perturbations induced by the simulated digital modulation and channel via Gumbel-Softmax relaxation. Building on this, we design a Bi-Level Meta-Evolution Mechanism (Bi-MEM) that treats manifold drift as a sequence of rapid adaptation tasks, aligning the codebook via data-level smoothing and parameter-level meta-updates. Furthermore, we validate MetaCom under AWGN, Rayleigh, and Rician fading channels. Experimental results demonstrate that MetaCom achieves superior trade-offs between perception precision and communication overhead, outperforming state-of-the-art methods by a significant margin under low-SNR conditions.

Abstract: Model broadcast in federated learning (FL) with wireless channels faces significant challenges due to large model sizes and heterogeneous, unreliable channel conditions. While rateless coding has been widely adopted in modern broadcast communications to handle channel heterogeneity, its potential for model broadcast in FL remains largely unexplored. To fill this gap, we propose a joint source-channel coding scheme based on rateless coding to improve the robustness and efficiency of downlink model broadcast in FL. Specifically, we establish a rigorous convergence analysis of FL to inform the rateless code and quantization design under noisy downlink channel conditions. Moreover, by observing that the iterative process of FL naturally provides prior information about client updates, we leverage client-side local models as conditional prior information to aid the decoding process, thereby reducing transmission costs. Extensive simulations demonstrate that our rateless coding scheme significantly enhances downlink model broadcast efficiency in FL, while retaining comparable training accuracies for both homogeneous and heterogeneous data distributions.

</details>

Degree-discrepancy profiles for every $k\le 28$ support the scaling $\max_r Δ(r)=Θ(\sqrt{N})=Θ(2^{k/2})$. The tabulated peaks are maxima on a discrete grid, hence lower bounds on the exhaustive maximum. The normalized peaks $d_k=\max_{r\in R_k}Δ(r)/2^{k/2}$ oscillate between values near 1/2 and values that approach, but do not exceed, 1. The data suggest $\liminf d_k=1/2$ and $\limsup d_k\le 1$. We conjecture the same square-root law for the exhaustive maximum, with those normalized envelopes. A Brownian-bridge heuristic accounts for the square-root scale. The same profiles do not place the recorded maximizer at mid-path: after folding by the complement identity $Δ(M-r)=Δ(r)$, the median of $\min(ρ^*_k,1-ρ^*_k)$ is 0.35, and several $k$ (notably 16 and 28) peak on a short window. Mid-horizon $Δ$ remains a large fraction of the peak, so the square-root scale can still come from a Brownian-bridge envelope. The same range has two readings of the starting offset: a pairing fragment in serial-knockout scheduling, and a change to the plaintext on an LFSR orbit. The location of the maximizer is not part of the conjecture. Open questions that separate the conjecture from a theorem are listed.

The proposed design method uses Bayesian optimization and samples from a subspace of the design space. The novel design method does not require knowledge about the channel or the modulation format and uses the frame error rate (FER) as loss function. The design method is applicable to irregular low-density parity-check (LDPC) codes with puncturing and shortening. Furthermore, the design method creates a set of iteration-agnostic LUTs that can be used over all iterations, which makes this approach suitable for fully node-parallel decoder architectures. Abstract: We derive closed-form Gaussian information projections of Beta--Gaussian distributions whose tangential components are supported on affine embedded hypercubes and whose normal components are Gaussian. The optimal Gaussian is uniquely characterized by moment matching, and the corresponding minimum Kullback--Leibler divergence admits an explicit expression depending only on the intrinsic Beta parameters, independently of the embedding geometry, ambient dimension, and normal Gaussian parameters. In the uniform case, this divergence reduces to a linear function of the intrinsic dimension. We further extend this construction to finite, pure embedded cubical complexes by considering mixtures of the componentwise Gaussian projections. Using the joint convexity of the Kullback--Leibler divergence, we establish an explicit upper bound on the approximation error of the resulting Gaussian Mixture. For uniform tangential distributions and volume-based mixture weights, the bound is linear in the intrinsic dimension and independent of the geometry of the complex, its embedding, and the normal Gaussian parameters.

Abstract: We study the average one-guess success probability of an adversary who observes the ordinary integer sum of a finite-support secret and an independent integer-valued mask, subject to an absolute-moment budget on the mask. For a uniform secret on an interval the exact optimum and an explicit optimiser are obtained. For a biased bit the masks that achieve no increase over the prior baseline are characterised and their minimal cost is determined. The entire cost-vulnerability frontier is then determined for every bias. It is the piecewise-linear interpolation of an explicit sequence of points generated by cost-optimally shifted truncated geometric masks. It has infinitely many affine pieces, which accumulate at the critical cost, where the frontier meets the prior baseline with zero slope. The proof rests on a layer-cake decomposition of an arbitrary mask into truncated geometrics, along which both the vulnerability and the cost are exactly additive. The results give exact design guidance for additive integer noise under the average one-guess metric; they do not constitute a cryptographic masking scheme.

</details>

Abstract: We give an information-theoretic formulation of two-stage secret sharing for situations in which secret information becomes available progressively. In the proposed model, shares for an initial part of the secret are distributed before the remaining part becomes available, and additional shares are distributed to new participants after the remaining part becomes available, without updating the shares already distributed and without using any additional information other than shares. To characterize this setting, we propose the notion of a two-stage access structure and provide its characterization. We further quantify the amount of information about the first-stage secret that the dealer must retain between the two distribution stages, derive a universal lower bound, and present a construction that achieves this bound for every two-stage access structure. We also position advance sharing as a special case of the proposed model and give its characterization and a related construction in classical information-theoretic secret sharing.

</details>

Abstract: Constructing codes that attain the Gilbert-Varshamov (GV) bound while admitting efficient encoding and decoding is a fundamental problem in coding theory. We study concatenated codes that reuse a single binary inner code across all outer coordinates. To exploit existing encoding and decoding algorithms, we restrict the outer codes to random subcodes of scaled copies of prescribed algebraic codes and investigate their minimum distance. Our first contribution is a general upper bound on the minimum-distance failure probability that separates two terms controlled by the inner code's bias moments and the dual weight distribution of the prescribed code, together with sufficient conditions for exponential decay. Our second contribution is a concrete construction satisfying these conditions using algebraic-geometric codes from the Garcia-Stichtenoth tower. For every sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$, we construct a family of random binary codes $C_m$ whose rates approach $\varepsilon^2$ and satisfy \[ \Pr\left[δ(C_m)\geq\frac12-\sqrt{\frac{\ln 2}{2}}\left(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon^3}{2}\right)\right]\geq 1-2^{-c\varepsilon^4N_m}, \] for all sufficiently large $m$, where $N_m$ is the block length and $c>0$ is independent of $\varepsilon$ and $m$. Thus, the distance guarantee matches the leading coefficient of the GV bound at low rates. For each fixed $\varepsilon$, the construction uses a single inner encoding map throughout the family and admits polynomial-time encoding and unique decoding within a guaranteed radius. This answers the question of Doron, Mosheiff, and Wootters for subcodes of scaled algebraic geometry codes at low rates.

Abstract: Rate-distortion functions are defined by a variational problem whose analytic solution depends on the source distribution and distortion measure. We develop a posterior-based construction framework using conditional entropy as a common distortion coordinate. Building on the rate-entropy formulation, we establish entropy-level attainability for discrete sources with finite entropy and absolutely continuous sources with integrable log densities. We give a constructive rate-entropy coding proof for finite-alphabet sources and such continuous sources, without a second-moment assumption. Marginal compatibility ensures that a candidate posterior and output distribution recover the source, while a Gibbs representation and a kernel mass bound certify attainment of maximum conditional entropy. The negative log posterior identifies candidate fixed distortions. Excess rate equals a conditional entropy gap and decomposes into budget slack and posterior mismatch. Convolution decompositions and conjugate posteriors provide two construction paths. They recover established results, including the Gamma-source rate-distortion function under Itakura-Saito distortion, and yield a closed-form parametric expression for zeta sources under divisibility-constrained logarithmic multiplicative distortion. Exact expressions are obtained on specified intervals for negative binomial sources under one-sided counting distortion and Gamma sources under one-sided mean error. A residual-count coordinate for Poisson entropy budgets and rate-entropy parametrizations of Gamma and Dirichlet counting channels connect model parameters to remaining uncertainty. The framework unifies distortion selection, compatible-channel construction, and optimality verification.

Abstract: The Gilbert-Varshamov (GV) bound is a fundamental benchmark for the existence of error-correcting codes. For non-linear $q$-ary codes of code length $n$, its finite length form is known to admit a multiplicative improvement of order $Θ(n)$. In contrast, for $q$-ary linear codes of arbitrary rates, the best previously known general improvement was only of order $Θ(\sqrt n)$. This leaves open whether the algebraic constraint of linearity inherently prevents a linear-order refinement. We show that it does not. For every fixed prime power $q$ and relative distance $0<δ<1-1/q$, we obtain a $Θ(n)$-multiplicative improvement of the GV bound for $q$-ary linear codes of arbitrary rate, matching the corresponding result for nonlinear codes. Our proof is based on a simple change of viewpoint. Rather than requiring the initial random linear construction to contain no low-weight vectors, we allow $O(n)$ low-weight points, show that they are independent in the underlying projective space, and then eliminate all of them simultaneously at the cost of a single dimension. The key probabilistic ingredient is an exponential bound on nontrivial linear dependencies among low-weight vectors. We further show that this approach can be extended to quantum codes despite the symplectic self-orthogonality constraint. We obtain a $Θ(n)$-multiplicative improvement of the quantum GV bound for every fixed $0<δ<1-1/q^2$ satisfying $H_{q^2}(δ)<1/2$. Thus, the same $Θ(n)$ finite length improvement is applicable to both general linear structure and symplectic self-orthogonality.

</details>

Abstract: The performance of Zak-OTFS modulation depends critically on the delay-Doppler (DD) domain pulse shaping filter, which must support both accurate channel estimation and reliable data detection. Filters reported in the Zak-OTFS literature fall short in bit error rate (BER) performance for higher-order modulation due to sensitivity to model-free channel estimation errors and pilot-data interference. Motivated by this, in this paper, we introduce a separable two-dimensional Mirabbasi-Martin (MM) filter for Zak-OTFS and propose a filter coefficient optimization framework that minimizes the aggregate sidelobe energy while preserving the compact structure of the MM prototype. By trading exact Nyquist nulls for high localization, the optimized MM filter exhibits no sidelobes with the narrowest $-40$ dB DD domain pulsewidth compared to sinc, Gaussian, and GS pulses. This compactness of the optimized filter tightly confines the pilot energy within the pilot region of the embedded frame, ensuring that the full response is captured for model-free channel estimation while significantly reducing pilot-data interference. This yields both an improved estimation performance and a significantly improved BER performance for higher-order QAM. For the proposed filter, we derive closed-form expressions for the effective DD domain channel and the covariance of the receive-filtered noise, which drastically reduce simulation run times. Our simulation results demonstrate that the optimized MM filter achieves significantly better uncoded/coded BER performance for higher-order QAM compared to that of sinc, Gaussian, and GS filters. For example, for 64-QAM with M=32, N=48, rate-1/2 convolutional code, and TDL-A channel model with fractional DDs, the optimized MM filter achieves a coded BER of 3x10^{-4} at an E_b/N_0 of 40 dB, while the sinc, Gaussian, and GS filters exhibit error floors above 10^{-2}.

</details>

Abstract: In a nonergodic channel such as a slowly fading link, the channel law is drawn once and held fixed for the whole codeword, and the relevant performance measure is the $\varepsilon$-capacity. We study the $\varepsilon$-capacity regions of two-user mixed multiple-access channels (MACs) with finite alphabets, additive cost constraints and an arbitrary mixing measure. The direct parts are derived from a single information spectrum formula; the converse parts use, in addition, Fano's inequality and Ahlswede's strong converse applied to each component. Without knowledge of the realized component, we prove a single-letter inner bound for every $0\le\varepsilon<1$, in which one input serves all components; for $\varepsilon=0$ it is tight, and the capacity region is given by the essential infima of the component mutual informations. We prove a single-letter outer bound, the outage region, in which each component may use its own input; it holds with or without channel state information (CSI) at either terminal, and it is the $\varepsilon$-capacity region when the encoders know the component and the parameter space is countable. Both bounds coincide, in all four CSI scenarios, for well-ordered mixed MACs, which include degradedly ordered families and mixtures with a common optimal input, such as mixed additive MACs. Without CSI the outer bound can be strictly loose, and the no-CSI $\varepsilon$-capacity region for $0<\varepsilon<1$ remains open in general. Finally, we extend Ahlswede's strong converse to $K$ users with cost constraints and show that most of these results extend to $K$-user mixed MACs.

</details>

From them we derive the capacity region of the general $K$-user MAC with cost constraint and show that it contains the polymatroid capacity region of the $K$-user Gaussian MAC. Consequently, the strong converse for the $K$-user Gaussian MAC, in the sense that the error probability tends to one for every rate tuple outside the capacity region, follows directly from the converse lemma, without the wringing technique and without hypothesis-testing meta-converses. This strong converse was first established by Fong and Tan, whose per-blocklength bounds yield it, through wringing and quantization. Abstract: Motivated by quantum key distribution networks, we compare key relay protocols with secure network coding on the same undirected graph. In this comparison, we fix the finite field, terminal set, and wiretap family. Each edge supplies one independent uniform symbol to its endpoints for key relay, or carries one symbol once for network coding. The network code may choose its source terminal, edge orientations, and causal transmission order before execution. In this setting, the standard one-time-pad simulation readily converts every secure network code to key relay. We investigate the reverse conversion to linear network coding. For two terminals and a one-symbol key, arbitrary key relay is equivalent to scalar linear acyclic network coding over the prescribed field. The proof uses connectivity and flow conservation, related to the binary $st$-flow protocol. However, this equivalence fails in general when several terminals seek one common key. A doubled triangle separates key relay from acyclic coding but admits causal coding on a cyclic orientation. On the four-terminal complete graph, a linear key relay protocol over the ternary field withstands the three perfect-matching wiretap sets, whereas no linear causal network code distributes a source symbol of the same size, even with private randomness at every vertex and its use in decoding. We also establish a linear key relay normal form that removes interaction and private randomness and bounds public communication. All results concern one-shot feasibility with exact agreement and perfect secrecy.

This matches the leading redundancy term of Levenshtein's classical existential bound. Building on the standard graph-coloring approach, we use a prime-based multicoloring of the edit-confusion graph to assign multiple colors to each $q$-ary word of length $m<n$. Appending a short, edit-protected representation of an assigned color to the length-$m$ word yields a length-$n$ codeword. The choice among the assigned colors carries additional message information, thereby reducing the redundancy. The construction admits deterministic encoding and decoding in $\widetilde O_{q,t}(n^{6t+2})$ and $\widetilde O_{q,t}(n^{7t+2})$ time, respectively.

Abstract: Per-pixel crop classification in satellite images is an important pre-processing step in remote crop monitoring. Leveraging progress in signed graph signal processing, we build an interpretable and lightweight neural network for binary crop classification (e.g., corn versus non-corn) in two stages: i) learn a signed graph from data, and ii) unroll a Lanczos-based eigensolver to compute the eigenvector $\u_1$ associated with the smallest eigenvalue of a hybrid graph Laplacian $\cL$ for spectral clustering. Specifically, we first construct a near-balanced signed graph $\cG^s$, where signed edge weights $w_{i,j}\in(-1,1)$ are computed from learned feature distances $d_{i,j}$ via a hyperbolic tangent mapping. Given $\cG^s$, we estimate $\u_1$ by unrolling the Lanczos algorithm and use $\mathrm{sign}(\u_1)$ for binary classification. Mathematically, we develop a theorem establishing conditions under which $\u_1$ can be well approximated after finite Lanczos iterations. Motivated by this theorem, we relax the classical Lanczos orthogonality constraints during unrolling and optimize end-to-end classification performance while promoting the derived conditions for accurate eigenvector approximation. Experiments on real-world Harmonized Landsat-Sentinel imagery show classification performance comparable to state-of-the-art deep learning models while reducing parameters by up to two orders of magnitude.

Abstract: Pinching antenna systems (PASS) have recently emerged as a promising architecture for reconfigurable, flexible and proximity-driven signal delivery. In this paper, we propose a segmented waveguide-based PASS architecture for enabling secure uplink communications, where each segment activates at most one pinching antenna (PA), which inherently eliminates the inter-antenna re-radiation effect and yields a tractable uplink signal model. To protect the uplink transmission against eavesdropping, the base station (BS) injects artificial noise (AN) toward the eavesdroppers via selected downlink segments using a duplex-mode segment selection mechanism, which dynamically configures each segment for either uplink reception or downlink AN transmission through switch-controlled fiber connections. We formulate the secrecy energy efficiency (SEE) maximization problem as a mixed-integer nonlinear program (MINLP) that jointly optimizes the receive beamforming vector, AN covariance, user transmit power, pinching antenna positions, and duplex-mode segment assignment. Given the non-convex and combinatorial nature of the problem, we develop an efficient alternating optimization (AO) framework, where the resulting subproblems are solved using the Dinkelbach method combined with successive convex approximation (SCA) and penalty-based relaxation. Simulation results demonstrate that the proposed framework significantly outperforms benchmark schemes in terms of SEE and robustness against waveguide attenuation. Moreover, AN effectively suppresses information leakage, while PA mobility, duplex-mode segment allocation, and waveguide segmentation further improve secrecy performance through flexible positioning and reduced in-waveguide propagation loss.

</details>

Abstract: The flexible configurability of pre-chirp parameters in affine frequency division multiplexing (AFDM) provides new degrees of freedom for designing covert communication mechanisms. Leveraging this property, this paper proposes a covert AFDM pre-chirp spreading index modulation (IM) framework referred to as AFDM-PCS-IM, tailored for high-mobility scenarios. Specifically, an orthogonal pre-chirp parameter codebook is constructed by exploiting the tunability of the pre-chirp parameters. To enhance covertness, the legitimate channel state information (CSI) is utilized to generate controlled perturbations that encrypt the codebook, thereby tightly coupling the modulation structure with instantaneous channel characteristics. The encrypted pre-chirp codebook is then employed as an IM dimension for information transmission, where the index bits are conveyed through the selection of pre-chirp parameter sequences. Since eavesdroppers cannot obtain consistent CSI, they are unable to reconstruct the encrypted codebook and thus cannot reliably recover any useful information. Moreover, to address channel estimation errors inherent in practical communication environments, a noise-aware CSI quantizer is further introduced to improve robustness under imperfect CSI conditions. Simulation results demonstrate that the proposed AFDM-PCS-IM framework with the TLMP-WC detector achieves superior covertness, improved bit error rate (BER) performance, and reduced computational complexity compared with the state-of-the-art schemes.

Abstract: Radio positioning needs the positions of its anchors, access points, base stations, or reconfigurable intelligent surfaces, and manual surveying is costly and error-prone. Calibrating this infrastructure from its own measurements is one estimation problem, studied in at least four communities as self-calibration, auto-positioning, anchor self-localization, network localization, or radio simultaneous localization and mapping. This tutorial teaches the problem through one factor graph. The measurement topology sets its factors, the reference information enters as priors, and other measurement models add nuisance states. The tutorial shows what ranges can identify, from rigidity to the motion a mobile device must contribute, and presents multidimensional scaling, nonlinear least squares, recursive filters, and convex relaxations as inference engines over this graph. One session of a ten-anchor ultra-wideband deployment is carried through every step, and recordings from a second, harsher deployment and a public partial mesh serve as contrasts. The examples illustrate three lessons. On a full mesh, choose a robust loss and set its scale first, and calibrate with the engine of the positioning system, since the engines then reach similar errors. On thinned or partial meshes, test the result at surveyed check points, because the start decides which minimum a solver reaches. On a line-of-sight deployment, estimate range offsets, which lower the error floor that a systematic ranging bias sets and a robust loss cannot remove. A decision tree and a table of their effects turn them into deployment guidance. Code and result tables are openly released, and the data are published separately.

</details>

Abstract: Compared to classical task-agnostic communication systems, which focus on reliable transmission of a message, semantic communication (SemCom) focuses on transmitting the task-relevant meaning behind the message. In this paper, we design a SemCom system for a distributed multi-view scenario, where multiple semantic devices observe a scene and collaborate through a single semantic receiver to execute a task. We propose adopting an Over-the-Air Computation (AirComp) channel for efficient use of resources, as AirComp uses a superposition of signals to directly compute a function, which in SemCom is decoding the semantic variable. However, AirComp relies heavily on perfect time and phase synchronization, which is not practical in real-world communication. We show that timing offsets of standards like 5G cause negligible degradation, and that larger timing offsets can be compensated by training. Nevertheless, the system cannot deal with the encountered levels of phase synchronization errors, even when trained with those levels of synchronization errors. Therefore, we propose integrating existing phase synchronization algorithms into the AirComp SemCom system training. Simulation results show that the proposed system can approach the task execution performance of a perfectly synchronized SemCom system.

Abstract: Orthogonal time frequency space (OTFS) modulation offers robust performance under high-mobility scenarios. Spread pilots distribute pilot energy across the delay-Doppler grid and are superimposed with data symbols, enabling full-grid resource utilization. However, pilot-data separation challenges the design of the real-time receiver pipeline: cross-ambiguity in channel sensing incurs high computational complexity, and pilot-data interference cancellation requires repeated processing through turbo iterations. Abstract: Orthogonal frequency division multiplexing (OFDM) is widely adopted in modern wireless systems due to its high spectral efficiency and robustness to multipath propagation. However, its performance can be severely degraded by narrowband and wideband interference, particularly in spectrally congested environments where interference overlaps significantly in time and frequency with the desired signal. In this paper, we investigate interference mitigation for OFDM signals using the FREquency-SHift (FRESH) filter, which exploits cyclostationarity inherent to the waveform or induced by the modulation design. By leveraging correlations between frequency-shifted versions of the received signal, the FRESH filter provides optimal linear filtering for the cyclostationary processes. We first analyze the second-order statistics of OFDM signals with known structure and characterize their cyclostationary properties. Based on this analysis, we derive a compact closed-form matrix expression for the optimal FRESH filter coefficients under the minimum mean-square error (MMSE) criterion. The proposed framework enables offline computation of the filter coefficients when the signal structure is known and interference parameters are estimated, thereby reducing real-time implementation complexity. Numerical results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method in suppressing interference and improving detection performance compared to conventional approaches.

</details>

Abstract: This letter investigates flexible intelligent metasurface (FIM)-aided secure multiple-input single-output (MISO) communications in the presence of a multi-antenna eavesdropper. By exploiting the morphing capability of the FIM, the transmit array geometry can be dynamically reconfigured to reshape the legitimate and eavesdropping channels. We formulate a secrecy rate maximization (SRM) problem by jointly optimizing the transmit covariance matrix and the FIM surface shape. For a given FIM surface shape, the optimal transmit covariance matrix is shown to be rank one and is obtained in closed form from the dominant generalized eigenvector. For a fixed transmit covariance matrix, a projected gradient ascent (PGA) method is developed to optimize the FIM shape. Based on these two subproblems, a block coordinate ascent (BCA) algorithm is proposed. Moreover, for the special line-of-sight (LoS) scenario, we derive a globally optimal closed-form joint transmit covariance matrix and FIM surface shape design under a sufficient morphing-range condition. Numerical results demonstrate that FIM effectively improves the secrecy rate over a conventional rigid antenna array (RAA). Moreover, FIM achieves a transmit-power saving of about 2.5 dB compared with the RAA benchmark at the same secrecy rate.

PATBO combines regularized zero-forcing (RZF) communication directions and spherical-wave near-field sensing directions with learned stream-power allocation. A token-preserving Transformer maintains the correspondence between individual users/targets and their allocated powers, while a Cramér--Rao bound (CRB)-sensitivity-aware objective emphasizes power-allocation errors with greater localization impact. The complete hybrid framework achieves $99.89\%$ reference-rate retention and $100\%$ constraint satisfaction on the retained test set, while requiring optimizer fallback for only $12.21\%$ of the scenarios. Direct PATBO inference requires $1.11$~ms per sample, corresponding to approximately $858\times$ and $779\times$ lower runtime than the evaluated cold-start and PATBO-warm-start sequential quadratic programming (SQP) implementations, respectively.

Abstract: Accurate urban radio map prediction is challenging due to complex building geometries and sophisticated electromagnetic interactions. To address this, we propose a data- and physics-driven framework that decouples wave propagation from environmental feature extraction. The core idea is to predict a sparse set of low-dimensional Secondary Reradiation Points (SRPs), which, together with a closed-form renderer deduced from electromagnetic theory, yields the final radio map. By learning these SRPs end-to-end rather than directly regressing the high-dimensional map, the neural network focuses on a compact physical parameterization, significantly improving accuracy and generalization. Numerical experiments demonstrate that our framework outperforms all seven baselines and consistently yields the lowest errors across all building-coverage quintiles, underscoring its robustness in dense, heavily obstructed urban environments.

Abstract: A general-purpose linear regression solver for a class of smooth loss functions is presented. The method applies to loss functions of the Moreau envelope type, which are continuously differentiable and have Lipschitz-continuous first derivatives. This class includes piecewise-defined cost functions consisting of constant, linear and quadratic segments, such as Huber's loss, insensitive least squares, one-sided least squares. The convergence guarantee provided by the associated proximal operator, the simplicity of the method and its versatility in handling various cost functions within a unified framework are its main advantages. MATLAB code for reproducing the numerical results presented in this article is provided.

Abstract: Full-space covariance optimization is costly in large-array Bayesian integrated sensing and communication (ISAC). A fixed transmit subspace can reduce this cost, but the resulting optimality loss depends on the posterior distribution. In this paper, a certified dynamic subspace method is developed for angle Bayesian Cramér-Rao bound (BCRB) minimization under joint angle and complex-gain uncertainty. For uniform linear arrays, exact response preservation over a nondegenerate posterior angular interval requires the full antenna space. Subspace sufficiency is therefore assessed through full-space objective-gap bounds. A communication-feasible basis is initialized from the user-channel span and local BCRB sensitivity. Restricted semidefinite programs are then solved. Primal restoration and dual repair yield gap bounds that remain valid after lossless user-beam recovery. When an accurate restricted solve does not certify the requested tolerance, the basis is expanded using a direction identified from the covariance dual slacks. Under a strict communication-power margin, joint uniqueness of the restricted optimal dual multipliers is established. With exact restricted solves, this uniqueness guarantees strict BCRB improvement after certificate-driven expansion. Numerical results validate the bounds against full-space references for the sampled problems. Compared with a fixed Fisher ordering, the proposed method can reach the same certificate target with fewer dimensions. On large arrays, total runtime is reduced relative to the full-space and factorized baselines at matched certificate targets.

</details>

Abstract: The "sim-to-real" gap introduced by hardware impairments motivates realistic experimental platforms for AI-native research in Non-Terrestrial Networks (NTN). This paper presents an open-source UAV-to-Ground measurement testbed built on the OpenAirInterface (OAI) stack and commodity Software Defined Radios (SDRs), enabling direct extraction of raw Sub-6 GHz Channel State Information (CSI) synchronized with flight telemetry. Through real-world flight campaigns, we observe a velocity-bandwidth decoupling effect: under unguided, measurement-only ridge extraction, dominant spectral ridges deviate strongly from the theoretical kinematic Doppler trajectory, whereas guided tracking substantially reduces this mismatch. This indicates that Doppler information is present, but not dominant, in the raw observable dynamics of commodity SDR-based measurements. We further benchmark representative deep learning predictors on this hardware-inclusive dataset and show, through a case study, that their behavior differs substantially from idealized expectations. By open-sourcing the testbed and dataset, we provide a realistic foundation for training and evaluating AI models for practical aerial network deployments.

Abstract: Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) links can change faster than route-specific channel data can be collected, so a channel predictor must often be adapted from only a short target-flight calibration segment. This paper studies measurement-calibrated synthetic pretraining as an offline source prior for rapid adaptation of channel state information (CSI) predictors. The synthetic source is calibrated to flight-only power evolution, temporal shape variation, frequency selectivity, and phase disturbance, and is then combined with measured source-flight fine-tuning before target-flight adaptation. Under chronological held-out flight splits, synthetic-to-real (S2R) fine-tuning reduces the measured-data budget needed to reach useful prediction in the low-data regime. For 50 ms-ahead prediction, it crosses a latest-channel-estimate persistence baseline with 50 measured training windows, roughly 0.5 s of 10 ms-spaced supervised windows, whereas training from scratch requires 600 windows. A target-prefix adaptation study further shows that a lightweight gated recurrent unit predictor can reuse the pretrained source prior with one prediction-layer update epoch, positioning measurement-calibrated synthetic pretraining as a reusable prior for scarce target-flight measurements.

Abstract: Protection of converter-dominated distribution networks is challenging due to limited fault currents, operating-mode transitions, bidirectional power flow, and heterogeneous network configurations. This paper proposes a scale-invariant neuromorphic protection framework to enable transferable protection decisions across such networks. The framework integrates asset-based normalization, multi-domain disturbance representation, event-driven spike encoding, leaky integrate-and-fire (LIF) dynamics, adaptive threshold regulation, and directional supervision within a unified mathematical formulation. Local voltage, current, active power, and reactive power measurements are transformed into dimensionless disturbance representations, while latency encoding and membrane integration capture disturbance intensity and temporal evolution. The framework is evaluated across four heterogeneous network configurations: a two-DER inverter-interfaced microgrid, an islanded ring microgrid, the IEEE 13-node feeder, and the CIGRE European medium-voltage benchmark. Across 480 cases, it achieves a median operating time of 7.6 ms, with 94.2 % of cases operating within one power cycle. The results demonstrate selective protection across different electrical ratings and network topologies using a common parameterization without network-specific retuning, highlighting the potential of neuromorphic processing for physically interpretable, communication-free protection of converter-dominated distribution networks.

Abstract: Reliable protection under varying power system operating conditions requires rapid fault detection while preserving selectivity against external faults and non-fault disturbances. This requirement is particularly challenging in communication-free schemes, where relays must determine trip conditions solely from local electrical measurements. To address this challenge, this paper proposes a directionally supervised neuromorphic protection scheme that integrates disturbance-responsive leaky integrate-and-fire dynamics with a direction-dependent firing threshold to distinguish internal faults from reverse-direction events. The unified relay architecture combines local disturbance detection, directional discrimination, and spike-based trip generation without requiring inter-relay communication. Its performance is evaluated under grid-connected and islanded conditions using a ring microgrid and the IEEE 13-node distribution feeder. Across 240 evaluated fault scenarios, the framework achieves median operating times of 5.4--7.6 ms across the two networks, with 78% of cases operating within 10 ms and 95% within 20 ms. It also maintains protection security during load disturbances, while fault-resistance analysis confirms its response under high-resistance faults. Controller-hardware-in-the-loop (CHIL) experiments using an RTDS simulator and an external dSPACE controller demonstrate trip-command assertion in approximately 10 ms and 5 ms for the tested single-line-to-ground and three-phase-to-ground faults, respectively. The results establish real-time feasibility within the evaluated conditions and characterize operating-time variability across fault types and resistance levels for different network configurations.

Abstract: This paper examines the performance degradation of receivers caused by imperfect channel state information (CSI), arising from training-based and semi-blind channel estimation techniques. We focus on the uplink of massive multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) systems operating in time-division duplexing (TDD) mode, considering the asymptotic regime where the number of base station (BS) antennas grows large while the number of users remains fixed. Leveraging random matrix theory (RMT), we analyze the asymptotic behavior of the signal-to-interference-plus-noise ratio (SINR) under linear MRC and MMSE detection schemes. We derive closed-form approximations of the achievable rates, prove their asymptotic tightness, and validate their accuracy through simulations for systems of finite dimensions. Numerical results under realistic non-terrestrial network (NTN) conditions further demonstrate that semi-blind channel estimation enables MRC and MMSE receivers to outperform their training-based counterparts, while also quantifying the number of pilot symbols required to achieve a desired SINR level.

Abstract: Cell-free massive MIMO (CF-mMIMO) can provide uniformly high system performance across the coverage area through distributed cooperation. However, keeping all antennas active leads to high circuit and fronthaul power consumption. This paper studies energy-efficient uplink reception through antenna activation, where only a subset of distributed antennas is selected to serve the users. To enable network-wide activation with low signaling overhead, each access point (AP) maps its locally estimated channel state information (CSI) into a lowdimensional channel-chart (CC) embedding and forwards this representation to the central processing unit (CPU) instead of the full CSI. Based on the received embeddings, we formulate a binary antenna-activation problem for network EE maximization and propose three low-overhead strategies: (i) direct embeddingdomain activation using a proxy EE metric without CSI reconstruction, (ii) nearest chart-point CSI approximation, which estimates CSI from a reference chart database for greedy EE optimization, and (iii) decoder-based CSI reconstruction using a pre-trained autoencoder. The proposed approaches further enable a systematic comparison of antenna activation techniques in terms of achieved performance, computational complexity, and signaling overhead. Numerical results show substantial EE gains over the all-active baseline, with only a minor degradation in sum spectral efficiency and a significant reduction in signaling overhead. Moreover, the proposed greedy framework achieves EE performance close to that of the simulated annealing baseline, confirming its effectiveness for low-complexity antenna activation.

</details>
